title: "Bill Cosby sexual assault cases"
---

# Bill Cosby sexual assault cases



[Bill Cosby](158894.md) in 1980

In 2014, multiple allegations emerged that [Bill Cosby](158894.md), a film, television, and stand-up comedy star often cited as a trailblazer for African Americans in the entertainment industry,[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3] had sexually assaulted dozens of women throughout his career.[^cite_note-4][^cite_note-5] Cosby was well known in the United States for the fatherly image he conveyed through his portrayal of [Cliff Huxtable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliff_Huxtable) in the sitcom *[The Cosby Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Cosby_Show)* (1984–1992) and gained a reputation as "America's Dad", but the allegations ended his career and sharply diminished his status as a pop culture icon. He received [numerous awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Bill_Cosby) and [honorary degrees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_honorary_degrees_awarded_to_Bill_Cosby) throughout [his career](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Cosby_filmography), many of which were revoked. There were previous allegations against Cosby, but they were dismissed and accusers were ignored or disbelieved.

Over 60 women have accused Cosby of [rape](7980471.md), [drug-facilitated sexual assault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug-facilitated_sexual_assault), [sexual battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_battery), [child sexual abuse](18721790.md), and [sexual harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_harassment). The earliest incidents allegedly took place in the mid-1960s. Assault allegations against Cosby became more public after a [stand-up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stand-up_comedy) routine by fellow comedian [Hannibal Buress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Buress) in October 2014, alluding to Cosby's covert sexual misbehavior; thereafter, many additional claims were made. The dates of the alleged incidents span from 1965 to 2008 in ten U.S. states and one [Canadian province](75763.md).[^cite_note-DailyBeastListofAccusersNov2014-6][^cite_note-ETOnlineListOfAccusersFeb2015-7][^cite_note-SlateListofAccusersNov2014-8] Cosby has maintained his innocence and repeatedly denied the allegations, but they nonetheless have effectively ended his career and destroyed his legacy. Amid the allegations, numerous organizations severed ties with Cosby and revoked honors and titles awarded to him. Media organizations pulled reruns of *The Cosby Show* and other television programs featuring Cosby from syndication. Ninety-seven colleges and universities rescinded honorary degrees.[^cite_note-Holley-9]

Most of the alleged acts fall outside the [statute of limitations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statute_of_limitations) for criminal legal proceedings, but criminal charges were filed against Cosby in one case and numerous [civil lawsuits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_lawsuit) were brought against him. As of November 2015, eight related civil suits were active against him.[^cite_note-WintonR-10][^cite_note-AIG_Lawsuit-11] [Gloria Allred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Allred) represented 33 of the alleged victims.[^cite_note-12] In July 2015, some court records were unsealed and released to the public from [Andrea Constand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Constand_v._Bill_Cosby)'s 2005 civil suit against Cosby, concerning a sexual assault in Cosby's home in January 2004; at that time, no criminal charges were filed. The full transcript of his [deposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deposition_\(law\)) was released to the media by a court reporting service. In his testimony, Cosby admitted to casual sex involving recreational use of the sedative-hypnotic [methaqualone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methaqualone) (Quaaludes) with a series of young women but with their full consent and knowledge, and he acknowledged that his dispensing of the prescription drug was illegal.[^cite_note-Moghe-13][^cite_note-NYT71815-14][^cite_note-Allred_33_AllegedVictims-15] In December 2015, three [Class II felony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felony#Classification_by_seriousness) charges of aggravated indecent assault were filed against Cosby in [Montgomery County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_County,_Pennsylvania), [Pennsylvania](23332.md),[^cite_note-bigstory.ap.org-16] based on allegations by Constand concerning incidents in January 2004. Cosby's first [trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Bill_Cosby) in June 2017 ended in a [mistrial](3276812.md).[^cite_note-17] Cosby was found guilty of three counts of aggravated indecent assault at retrial on April 26, 2018[^cite_note-18] and on September 25, 2018, he was sentenced to three to ten years in state prison and fined \$25,000 plus the cost of the prosecution, \$43,611.[^cite_note-\:0-19]

Cosby appealed on June 25, 2019, and the verdict was subsequently upheld and granted an appeal by the Pennsylvania Supreme Court.[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-appealfailed-21] On June 30, 2021, the Pennsylvania Supreme Court found that an agreement with previous prosecutor [Bruce Castor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Castor) prevented Cosby from being charged in the case, and overruled the conviction.[^cite_note-22] The Supreme Court's decision prevents him from being tried on the same charges a third time.[^cite_note-23] The Montgomery County district attorney's office filed a *[certiorari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Certiorari)* petition asking the U.S. Supreme Court to hear the case,[^cite_note-Montco_SCOTUS_cert_press_release-24] but on March 7, 2022, the petition was denied, making the decision of the state supreme court final.[^cite_note-SCPetition-25] Cosby's legal issues continued following his release from prison. In 2014, Judy Huth had filed a civil suit against Cosby in California, alleging that he had sexually assaulted her in 1975 at age 16. The trial began in 2022, and the jury ruled in Huth's favour. Cosby was ordered to pay \$500,000 in compensatory damages.[^cite_note-\:02-26] In 2023, nine women filed sexual assault suits against Cosby.[^cite_note-27] In 2026, Cosby was found liable for drugging and raping Donna Motsinger in 1972 and was ordered to pay her \$19.25 million for pain and suffering, plus \$40 million in [punitive damages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punitive_damages).[^cite_note-28]

## Background

### 1965–1996 allegations



Cosby in 1969

The earliest allegation against [Bill Cosby](158894.md) dates back to December 1965: in 2005, Kristina Ruehli[^cite_note-29] came forward as [Jane Doe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Doe) #12 in the [Andrea Constand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Constand_v._Bill_Cosby) case and alleged that Cosby drugged and assaulted her then in his [Beverly Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Hills,_California) home. Further, Ruehli said she told her boyfriend about the incident, and told her daughter in the 1980s.

In the early 1980s, Joan Tarshis told freelance reporter John Milward about an alleged sexual assault by Cosby. Milward did not write about the allegations.[^cite_note-washpost-30] In 1996, *[Playboy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playboy_Playmate)* [Playmate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playboy_Playmate) [Victoria Valentino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Valentino) gave a videotaped interview in which she made sexual assault allegations against Cosby. The interview was conducted for an exposé on the lives of *Playboy* models, which was never published.[^cite_note-washpost-30]

After the allegations resurfaced in 2014, [Wendy Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendy_Williams) recalled that during her radio show in 1990, she referred to sexual assault allegations against Cosby published in the *[National Enquirer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Enquirer)* tabloid. Williams said Cosby called her boss during the broadcast demanding that Williams be fired.[^cite_note-ferrier_enquirer-31][^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33]

### Later allegations and investigations (2000–2006)

Main article: [Andrea Constand v. Bill Cosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Constand_v._Bill_Cosby)

On February 1, 2000, according to a statement by Detective Jose McCallion of the [New York County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_County,_New_York) [District Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Attorney)'s [Special Victims Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Victims_Unit), Lachele Covington, who was 20 years old at the time, filed a criminal complaint against Cosby alleging that on January 28, 2000, at his Manhattan townhouse, he tried to put her hands down his pants and [exposed himself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indecent_exposure). Covington also alleged that Cosby grabbed her breasts and tried to put his hands down her pants. Cosby was questioned and insisted "it was not true." The [New York City Police Department](255413.md) (NYPD) referred her complaint to the D.A., but they declined to prosecute.[^cite_note-radaronline_secretly-34]

In January 2004, Andrea Constand, a former [Temple University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_University) employee, accused Cosby of drugging and fondling her; however, in February 2005, [Montgomery County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_County,_Pennsylvania), [Pennsylvania](23332.md)'s District Attorney said there would be no charges due to insufficient credible and admissible evidence.[^cite_note-cbc.ca_1.579908-35] Constand then filed a [civil claim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_claim) in March 2005, with [thirteen women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Women) as potential witnesses if the case went to trial.[^cite_note-cnn-36][^cite_note-WPost-37] Cosby [settled out of court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_settlement) for an undisclosed amount in November 2006.[^cite_note-cnn-36] After learning that charges were not pursued in the Constand case, California lawyer Tamara Lucier Green, the only publicly-named woman in the prior case, alleged in February 2005 that Cosby drugged and assaulted her in the 1970s.[^cite_note-WoolfGuardian-38][^cite_note-philadelphiamagazine-39][^cite_note-NYT-40] Cosby's lawyer said Cosby did not know her and that the events did not happen.[^cite_note-cnn2014-41]



Cosby in 2006

In a July 2005 *[Philadelphia Daily News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Daily_News)* interview, Beth Ferrier, an anonymous "Jane Doe" witness in the Constand case, alleged that in 1984 Cosby drugged her coffee and she awoke with her clothes partially removed.[^cite_note-MyUser_Foxnews.com_November_19_2014c-42] In 2005, Shawn Upshaw Brown, a woman with whom Cosby admitted to having an [extramarital affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extramarital_affair) in the 1970s, claimed in the *National Enquirer* that Cosby drugged and raped her the last time the two were together sexually. Brown is the mother of [Autumn Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autumn_Jackson), who claims to be Cosby's illegitimate daughter. Jackson was convicted in 1997 of [extortion](162449.md) after she threatened to make the claims public in the *[Globe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globe_\(tabloid\))* tabloid. In 2015, Brown went into more detail with her renewed allegations in an interview.[^cite_note-43]

On June 9, 2006, [*Philadelphia* magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_\(magazine\)) published an article by Robert Huber which gave graphic detail about Constand's allegations, and the similar stories told by Green and Ferrer about how they too were drugged and sexually assaulted. With these severe allegations against Cosby, Huber wrote: "His lawyers have gotten it pushed to the back burner, down to a simmer, and maybe it will amount to nothing, yet there is also the possibility that it will bubble up to destroy him."[^cite_note-44] The article was titled *Dr. Huxtable & Mr. Hyde*,[^cite_note-45] an allusion to both Cosby's character [Dr. Cliff Huxtable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliff_Huxtable) on *[The Cosby Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Cosby_Show)* and to a person having two starkly distinct "[Jekyll and Hyde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr_Jekyll_and_Mr_Hyde_\(character\))" personalities. This article also presented Barbara Bowman, who came forward after reading about Constand's story, saying she could not sit in silence any longer. Details of Bowman's similar drug and sexual assault allegations were published in the magazine's November 1, 2006, issue. Bowman reported two incidents occurring around early 1986, wherein she was eighteen years old and working as an aspiring model and actress after her agent introduced her to Cosby and he became a good friend and mentor, saying that she escaped his attacks, returned home to Denver, and Cosby thereafter subverted her career.[^cite_note-46]

### Hannibal Buress remarks (October 2014)



In a 2014 stand-up act, [Hannibal Buress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Buress) (pictured) publicly accused Cosby of rape.

On October 16, 2014, as part of a [stand-up comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stand-up_comedy) routine in Philadelphia, [Hannibal Buress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Buress) addressed Cosby's legacy of "talk\[ing] down"[^cite_note-PhillyMagBuressRoutineVideo-47] to young black men about their mode of dress and lifestyle. Buress criticized the actor's public moralizing by saying, "Yeah, but you rape women, Bill Cosby, so turn the crazy down a couple notches."[^cite_note-PhillyMagBuressRoutineVideo-47] When the audience appeared to respond to Buress's accusation as an incredulous joke, he encouraged everyone to search "Bill Cosby rape" on [Google](1092923.md).[^cite_note-cbs_buress-48]

Buress had used the same Cosby routine for the previous six months with little response,[^cite_note-cbs_buress-48] but word of the October performance spread rapidly after being posted on [*Philadelphia*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_\(magazine\)) magazine's website.[^cite_note-VultureTimelineOfAccusations-49][^cite_note-PhillyMagBuressRoutineVideo-47][^cite_note-LATimesReBuressRole-50] Media coverage intensified, with numerous publications tackling the question of how Cosby managed to maintain, as Buress called it in his routine, a "teflon image" despite over a decade of public sexual abuse accusations.[^cite_note-nbc_cosby_why-51][^cite_note-accusertable_bowman-52]

Shortly afterward, *[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)* reported that either Cosby or his representative posted a request for [Twitter](9988187.md) followers to "Go ahead. [Meme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_meme) me!" The tweet was deleted after a large number of submitted memes referenced the accusations against Cosby.[^cite_note-53]

### Additional assault allegations

After Buress's remarks came to the attention of journalist Joan Tarshis, in November 2014,[^cite_note-Hughes-54] model [Janice Dickinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janice_Dickinson),[^cite_note-Bueno-55] actress [Louisa Moritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisa_Moritz),[^cite_note-Fox_News-56] actor [Lou Ferrigno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lou_Ferrigno)'s wife Carla,[^cite_note-Fox_News-56] Florida nurse Therese Serignese,[^cite_note-huffpost-serignese-57] *Playboy* Playmates Valentino[^cite_note-d7074938-58] and Sarita Butterfield,[^cite_note-Butterfield-59] actress [Michelle Hurd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Hurd),[^cite_note-DailyBeastListofAccusersNov2014-6] and eleven other women[^cite_note-DailyBeastListofAccusersNov2014-6] also made accusations of assaults by Cosby committed against them between 1965 and 2004.[^cite_note-MyUser_The_New_York_Times_November_20_2014c-60] [Charlotte Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlotte_Laws) wrote a November 2014 article published by *[Salon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salon_\(website\))* accusing Cosby of assaulting a friend of hers, with whom she subsequently had lost contact.[^cite_note-Laws-61] The following month, in a *[Vanity Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_Fair_\(magazine\))* article, model [Beverly Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Johnson) alleged that she was drugged by Cosby during a 1986 [audition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audition), and that she knew other women with similar accounts.[^cite_note-Vanity_Johnson-62]

Cosby's attorney said Dickinson's account differed from her prior accounts of the incident and released a statement that said in part: "Mr. Cosby does not intend to dignify these allegations with any comment."[^cite_note-Littleton-63] A follow-up statement dismissed the allegations as "unsubstantiated" and an example of "media vilification".[^cite_note-MyUser_The_Washington_Post_November_23_2014z-64] A joint statement from Cosby and Constand, who had received a civil settlement in 2006, clarified the statement released a few days prior by stating that it did not refer to Constand's case, which was resolved years ago.[^cite_note-billcosby.com/2014/11/1744-65]

In January 2015, Cindra Ladd alleged that Cosby drugged and sexually assaulted her in 1969.[^cite_note-Fisher-66] In May 2015, Lili Bernard claimed that Cosby sexually assaulted her in the early 1990s, and that she was interviewed by police in [Atlantic City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City), [New Jersey](21648.md), regarding the allegation. Because New Jersey has no [statute of limitations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statute_of_limitations) for rape, Bernard hoped charges would be brought, but media reports noted "it wasn't clear \[...] if what \[Bernard] says happened to her happened in New Jersey."[^cite_note-Puente-67]

On July 27, 2015, *[New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_\(magazine\))* magazine's cover featured images of 35 women sitting in chairs with the last chair empty, suggesting there may be more victims who had not come forward yet. The 35 women told "their stories about being assaulted by Bill Cosby, and the culture that wouldn't listen." Eleven other women known to *New York* who alleged sexual assault by Cosby declined to be photographed and interviewed for the feature.[^cite_note-nymagexpose-68] According to *[Vox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vox_\(website\))*, the stories span "more than five decades" and are "remarkably similar, typically involving the comedian offering a woman a cup of coffee or some sort of alcoholic beverage—which may be spiked with drugs—and allegedly sexually assaulting the victim as she's impaired or unconscious."[^cite_note-Lopez-69]

On September 17, 2015, [A\&E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A\&E_\(TV_network\)) broadcast the documentary *Cosby: The Women Speak*, a program in which thirteen alleged victims were interviewed.[^cite_note-A\&E-70][^cite_note-Pilkington-71] By October 24, nearly sixty women had claimed Cosby had sexually abused them,[^cite_note-usatoday.com-72] and the terms "[sociopath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sociopathy)"[^cite_note-DailyBeastListofAccusersNov2014-6] and "[serial rapist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serial_rapist)"[^cite_note-DailyBeastListofAccusersNov2014-6][^cite_note-Holley-9][^cite_note-Hughes-54][^cite_note-jezebel_reading-73][^cite_note-Thistlethwaite-74][^cite_note-Goldberg_serial_rapist-75] were used to describe him. Cosby accuser Jewell Allison described him as a "sociopath"[^cite_note-DailyBeastListofAccusersNov2014-6] and stated: "We may be looking at America's greatest serial rapist that ever got away with this for the longest amount of time. He got away with it because he was hiding behind the image of Cliff Huxtable."[^cite_note-Holley-9]

### Table of accusers' allegations

Cosby has been accused by over[^cite_note-\:2-76] sixty women of [rape](7980471.md), [drug facilitated sexual assault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_facilitated_sexual_assault), [sexual battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_battery), [child sexual abuse](18721790.md), or other [sexual misconduct](457212.md). The earliest alleged incidents took place in the mid-1960s, with the rest covering the period up to 2008. In 2023, a new victim came out alleging to have been raped by Cosby.[^cite_note-\:2-76]

Cosby attorney Marty Singer stated, "There is virtually no standard by which the media are holding Mr Cosby's accusers \[...] Anyone and everyone who wants to file a suit or get on television can be guaranteed fawning coverage. The very same media have demonstrated an unconscionable disinterest in the veracity of his accusers and their motives."

Cosby has maintained his innocence and repeatedly denied the allegations. In November 2014, in response to a question about the allegations, Cosby said: "I don't talk about it."[^cite_note-maya-77] Cosby has declined to discuss publicly the accusations in past interviews.[^cite_note-KFORTV-78] However, he told *Florida Today*: "people shouldn't have to go through that and shouldn't answer to innuendos."[^cite_note-KFORTV-78] In May 2015, he said "I have been in this business 52 years and I've never seen anything like this. Reality is a situation and I can't speak."[^cite_note-theweek_555357-79]

## Criminal investigations

### Constand case

#### Unsealing of *Constand v. Cosby* deposition

Main article: [Andrea Constand v. Bill Cosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Constand_v._Bill_Cosby)

On July 8, 2015, Constand and her attorney Dolores Troiani filed a motion to negate the [confidentiality agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confidentiality_agreement) in the 2005 case against Cosby, claiming Cosby "total\[ly] abandon\[ed] the confidentiality portions of the agreement" by way of his recent, sweeping denials of all allegations against him.[^cite_note-legalintelligencer-109][^cite_note-torontosun_constant-110] A judge ruled that releasing the sealed documents was justified by Cosby's role as a "public [moralist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moral)" in contrast to his possible criminal private behavior.[^cite_note-MDale-111]

Although some of the files from the Constand case had been unsealed, the transcript of Cosby's several [depositions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deposition_\(law\)) was not among them. Instead, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* obtained the complete transcript from a court reporting service hired by Constand's attorney and released it to the public.[^cite_note-NYT72015-112] After it was discovered that the transcript of the deposition was released, Cosby's lawyers filed a new motion in the case on July 21, 2015, asserting that Constand and Troiani may have orchestrated the release.[^cite_note-theguardian_sanctions-113][^cite_note-Fox8_deposition-114]

In the court filing condemning the release of the deposition, Cosby's attorneys stressed that no testimony actually unsealed by a judge stated that he engaged in non-consensual sex or gave anyone Quaaludes without their knowledge or consent. "Reading the media accounts, one would conclude that the Defendant has admitted to rape," the document said. "And yet the Defendant admitted to nothing more than being one of the many people who introduced Quaaludes into their consensual sex life in the 1970s." Cosby's lawyers further contended that a court reporting service hired by Constand had released the 2005 court transcript to the *Times*, days earlier, in a "massive breach of protocol". The court reporters' code of ethics prohibits the release of testimony without all parties first being contacted.[^cite_note-theguardian_sanctions-113][^cite_note-Fox8_deposition-114]

In the deposition, Cosby denied any sexual assault of any women but admitted he had used [sedatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sedative) to help gain their cooperation.[^cite_note-115] He testified that he had obtained Quaaludes from [gynecologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gynecologist) Leroy Amar,[^cite_note-116] who knew Cosby had no intention of taking the drugs himself. Cosby instead intended to give them to women he wanted to have sexual relations with and admitted he had given the drug to at least one woman and other people.[^cite_note-117] Cosby admitted knowing it was illegal at the time to dispense the drug to other people. Amar would later have his [medical license](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_license) revoked in California and [New York State](8210131.md); he died in 2002.[^cite_note-Moghe-13][^cite_note-Dale-118]

#### 2015 criminal charges

On December 30, 2015, in Montgomery County, Pennsylvania, Cosby was charged with three counts[^cite_note-119] of [aggravated indecent assault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aggravated_indecent_assault) on Constand as a result of a single incident allegedly occurring at his home in [Cheltenham Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheltenham_Township) on an unspecified date between mid-January and mid-February 2004 (referred to by media coverage as "January 2004"), according to the detailed arrest warrant affidavit filed on December 29, 2015.[^cite_note-Kevin_R._Steele-120] These were the first criminal charges as a result of sexual assault allegations that had been made by many women against Cosby.[^cite_note-bigstory.ap.org-16]

Cosby was [arraigned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arraignment) that afternoon without entering a plea; his [bail](170652.md) was set at \$1 million. Cosby surrendered his [passport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passport), posted bond, and was escorted to the Cheltenham Township police station to be booked, fingerprinted, and photographed for a mug shot.[^cite_note-Castillo-121] The charges were based on Constand's statement to police of unwanted sexual contact (though not intercourse) in early 2004 that had first been reported to the [Durham Regional Police Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durham_Regional_Police_Service) near Constand's home in southern Ontario, Canada, on January 13, 2005;[^cite_note-Kevin_R._Steele-120] the report was forwarded to authorities in Pennsylvania.[^cite_note-The_Associated_Press-99] On February 17, 2005, then-district attorney [Bruce Castor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Castor) had released a statement that charges would not be brought at that time.

Constand launched a civil lawsuit against Cosby in 2005 which was confidentially settled in July 2006. Some testimony from that case was unsealed in July 2015.[^cite_note-6abc.com-122] Based on details in that testimony and new interviews with certain witnesses, newly elected District Attorney Kevin Steele decided to file charges on December 30, 2015.[^cite_note-Castillo-121] The criminal court documents allege[^cite_note-123] that blue pills, said to be [Benadryl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benadryl) by Cosby,[^cite_note-Kevin_R._Steele-120] were given to Constand, who had also been drinking wine during the January 2004 incident.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125]

Cosby's attorney issued a statement saying, "We intend to mount a vigorous defense against this unjustified charge and we expect that Mr. Cosby will be exonerated by a court of law."[^cite_note-Castillo-121] Cosby's attorneys filed a motion to dismiss the sexual assault charges in January 2016, stating that Castor's office promised in 2005 that Cosby would not be prosecuted.[^cite_note-126] In testimony involving Cosby's motion, Castor defended his decision not to bring charges, citing among other things Constand's year-long delay in reporting the allegations, her continued contact with Cosby, and suggestions that she and her mother might have tried to extort the TV star.[^cite_note-127]

On February 3, 2016, Judge Steven O'Neill ruled "there was no basis" to dismiss the case based on Cosby's assertions.[^cite_note-128] Cosby's legal team sought an [appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appeal) before the [Superior Court of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Court_of_Pennsylvania);[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] on April 25, 2016, the Superior Court refused to hear Cosby's appeal from the denial of his motion to dismiss, lifted a temporary stay of the pre-trial hearing, and sent the case back to the original court.[^cite_note-Cosby_loses_appeal-131] On April 13, 2016, Cosby filed a motion with the Superior Court to re-seal the deposition from the original Constand lawsuit. His lawyers made a similar request in federal court in [Massachusetts](1645518.md) earlier, but that motion was denied by Judge David H. Hennessy, who likened these efforts to putting the "toothpaste back in the tube" since Cosby's testimony had already been in the news for months.[^cite_note-Cosby_files_Motion_Constand_deposition_suppress-132]

At the preliminary hearing on May 24, a judge found that there was enough evidence to proceed with a trial despite the fact that Constand did not testify, which is allowed under Pennsylvania law. He set a pre-trial hearing for September 6. Cosby appealed this decision based upon the belief his legal team had the right to [cross-examine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-examination) the accuser; he lost this appeal on October 12. The [Pennsylvania Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Supreme_Court) announced it would review the state law in a separate case, to which Cosby's lead attorney, Brian McMonagle, said he would try to add Cosby's case in hopes of having it thrown out.[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] On September 6, 2016, Judge Steven O'Neill set a trial date for June 6, 2017.[^cite_note-Rappleye,_Connor-135] On April 12, 2017, the Pennsylvania Supreme Court declined to hear Cosby's appeal to question Constand before the trial.[^cite_note-Cosby_loses_Pennsylvania_Supreme_Court-136]

Cosby had faced a maximum of fifteen to thirty years in prison if found guilty on all three counts and a [fine](146699.md) up to \$25,000.[^cite_note-Gibbons-137] His trial started on June 5, 2017,[^cite_note-Rappleye,_Connor-135] and ended in [mistrial](3276812.md) on June 17.[^cite_note-mistrial-138]

### Judith Huth

On December 16, 2014, after a ten-day investigation, Los Angeles prosecutors declined to file charges against Cosby after Judith Huth claimed the comedian molested her around 1974 at the [Playboy Mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playboy_Mansion). Huth had met with Los Angeles police detectives for ninety minutes. In rejecting the case, prosecutors evaluated the charge Cosby would have faced in 1974. Prosecutors took into account legislative changes that extended the statute of limitations for certain crimes but found no way that Cosby could be legally prosecuted.[^cite_note-7online_molestation-139]

### Lili Bernard

On April 30, 2015, Cuban-American visual artist Lili Bernard filed a sexual assault complaint against Cosby in New Jersey, which has no statute of limitations for sexual assault.[^cite_note-Bernard-140] On July 1, 2015, prosecutors declined to prosecute Cosby because the alleged offense happened before 1996 (the year the law was changed to remove the statute of limitations).[^cite_note-inquisitor_not_face-141]

### Chloe Goins

From July through October 2015, the [Los Angeles Police Department](218985.md) (LAPD) conducted a criminal investigation into Cosby's alleged sexual assault of then 18-year-old model Chloe Goins.[^cite_note-LAPD-142] The following day, in a statement to [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)), the LAPD said it will explore any sexual assault accusations against Cosby, including accusations for which the statute of limitations has expired,[^cite_note-abcnews_LAPD_Confirms-143] and referred the case to the [Los Angeles County](79734.md) D.A.'s office.[^cite_note-la_da_review-144]

As with nearly all other cases of alleged sexual misconduct by Cosby, the statute of limitations has expired on this 2008 case, according to most experts. Nonetheless, an investigation of was opened because "LAPD officials said there were many reasons to investigate sexual assault allegations that fall beyond those legal deadlines. Old accusations may lead investigators to more recent incidents with other victims" and Goins would be allowed to testify if charges were laid in any other (more recent) cases.[^cite_note-Winton-145] Cosby's former attorney Martin Singer stated he would provide documentary evidence to the LAPD that established Cosby was in New York on August 9, 2008, the date of the incident alleged by Goins.[^cite_note-cnn.com-146]

On January 6, 2016, the Los Angeles District Attorney's office announced that no charge would be laid because they were "blocked by the statute of limitations or lacked sufficient evidence".[^cite_note-Almasy-101][^cite_note-Bowley-147]

## Trials

Main article: [Trial of Bill Cosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Bill_Cosby)

Cosby's first trial in June 2017 ended in a [mistrial](3276812.md).[^cite_note-148] Cosby was found guilty of three counts of aggravated indecent assault at retrial on April 26, 2018[^cite_note-149] and on September 25, 2018, he was sentenced to three to ten years in state prison and fined \$25,000 plus the \$43,611 cost of the prosecution.[^cite_note-\:03-150]

Cosby appealed on June 25, 2019, and the verdict was subsequently upheld and granted an appeal by the Pennsylvania Supreme Court.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-appealfailed2-152] On June 30, 2021, the Pennsylvania Supreme Court found that Cosby's agreement with previous prosecutor [Bruce Castor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Castor) prevented him from being charged in the case, and overturned the conviction.[^cite_note-153] The Supreme Court's decision prevents him from being tried on the same charges a third time.[^cite_note-154] The Montgomery County district attorney's office filed a *[certiorari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Certiorari)* petition asking the U.S. Supreme Court to hear the case,[^cite_note-Montco_SCOTUS_cert_press_release2-155] but on March 7, 2022, the petition was denied, making the decision of the state supreme court final.[^cite_note-SCPetition2-156]

## Civil lawsuits against Cosby

As of November 13, 2015, there were nine lawsuits pending against Cosby. Huth and Goins were both suing the actor for damages related to their alleged sexual assaults. Although the statute of limitations expired for most other accusers to sue directly for alleged assault, multiple accusers filed [defamation](28661.md) lawsuits claiming that Cosby called them liars throughout 2014. Dickinson, Hill, Ruehli, and McKee have filed individual lawsuits. Green, Serignese, Traitz, Bowman, Tarshis, Moritz, and Leslie are also involved in a combined lawsuit against Cosby. Most active lawsuits are currently on hold awaiting the outcome of his criminal trial. Some are still allowed to file motions and depose witnesses, with the exception of Cosby himself. Most judges have indicated that a civil trial will not take place until after the criminal trial.

In 2005, Constand sued Cosby. The parties settled the case on confidential terms in 2006. In July 2015, after portions of the sealed deposition were released, Cosby released a statement saying the "only reason" why he settled "was because it would have been embarrassing in those days to put all those women on the stand, and his family had no clue."[^cite_note-157] During summer 2015, both Cosby's legal team and Constand's lawyer Troiani filed motions accusing the other party of having broken the confidentiality agreement that was part of the 2006 settlement.[^cite_note-158] The primary issue alleged by Cosby was that a [court reporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_reporter) released the full transcript of his deposition, blaming Troiani for this act[^cite_note-159] and seeking sanctions against Troiani.[^cite_note-160] A federal judge had unsealed portions of the evidence from the 2005 lawsuit, but that did not include Cosby's statements made [under oath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Under_oath).[^cite_note-161]

The significance of the availability of the deposition is that it contains admissions made by Cosby about his tactics in dealing with other women, including the use of "powerful sedatives \[including Quaaludes] in a calculated pursuit of young women", according to a *New York Times* summary.[^cite_note-162] The transcript of the full testimony was obtained by the *Times* from a court reporting service, where it was publicly available. The significance of the availability of Cosby's deposition from the Constand case is that plaintiffs in other cases against Cosby, such as the Green, et al. defamation suit,[^cite_note-163] might be allowed by judges to use the transcript's contents as evidence now that its contents are widely known in the public.[^cite_note-164]

Thirty-three of the accusers were represented by Allred.[^cite_note-Allred_33_AllegedVictims-15] One accuser, Dickinson, was represented by Allred's daughter, [Lisa Bloom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_Bloom).[^cite_note-cnn_dickinson_lawsuit-165] On December 11, 2015, Allred stated in an interview with *Philadelphia* magazine that more alleged victims would be coming forward. She said, "More women have contacted me who have not yet spoken out publicly, some of whom may do so in the future and some of whom have chosen not to do so. They wanted me to know the information that they had so it would be of assistance to us and our victim. There are definitely more women who wish to speak out in the future who allege that they are victims of Mr. Cosby."[^cite_note-Allred_number_accusers_more_coming_forward-166]

### Andrea Constand

Constand filed a civil claim in March 2005, with thirteen women as potential witnesses if the case went to court.[^cite_note-cnn-36][^cite_note-WPost-37] Cosby settled out of court for an undisclosed amount in November 2006.[^cite_note-cnn-36] In a July 2005 *Philadelphia Daily News* interview, Beth Ferrier, one of the anonymous "Jane Doe" witnesses in the Constand case, alleged that in 1984 Cosby drugged her coffee and she awoke with her clothes partially removed.[^cite_note-MyUser_Foxnews.com_November_19_2014c-42] After learning that charges were not pursued in the case, Green, the only publicly named woman in the prior case, came forward with allegations in February 2005 that Cosby had drugged and assaulted her in the 1970s.[^cite_note-WoolfGuardian-38][^cite_note-philadelphiamagazine-39][^cite_note-NYT-40] Cosby's lawyer said he did not know her and the events did not happen.[^cite_note-cnn2014-41] It was revealed in 2018 that Cosby had settled with Constand for \$3.4 million.[^cite_note-167]

### Tamara Green, *et al.*

Green filed a lawsuit against Cosby and his representatives in December 2014, claiming that the continued denials amounted to publicly branding her as a liar, resulting in defamation of character.[^cite_note-green_lawsuit-168] Green's lawsuit was filed in Cosby's home state of Massachusetts. In January 2015, the lawsuit was amended to allow fellow accusers Traitz and Serignese to be added as co-plaintiffs.[^cite_note-169] Singer had released a statement specifically targeting Traitz after she posted allegations against Cosby on [Facebook](7529378.md) in November, calling her story "ridiculous", "absurd", and "utter nonsense".[^cite_note-people_singer_traitz-170] No such denials were leveled explicitly at Serignese, but she claims the sweeping denials against all Cosby's accusers included her and have damaged her reputation as well.[^cite_note-green_lawsuit_amended-171]

On October 20, 2015, [American International Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_International_Group) (AIG) filed legal papers to try to put a stay on the [litigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litigation), pending a court declaration of whether the insurance company had a [duty to defend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duty_to_defend) Cosby as well as pay for any actual damages won. AIG Property Casualty Company claimed that Cosby's policy did not cover the liability he is currently facing in the lawsuit—but that they have nonetheless been funding Cosby's legal defense "at a considerable cost".[^cite_note-aig_October20-172]

On November 13, 2015, it was reported that four more women—Bowman, Tarshis, Moritz, and Leslie—joined the lawsuit as additional plaintiffs. Bowman, Tarshis, and Leslie are represented by Chicago attorney Michael Bressler. Lawyers for Cosby declined to comment.[^cite_note-4_Women_Join_Suit-173] On December 14, Cosby filed a countersuit for defamation against the plaintiffs, claiming that they made "malicious, opportunistic and false and defamatory accusations of sexual misconduct against him". He also claims each defendant "knowingly published false statements and accusations".[^cite_note-Cosby_counter_sues-174] Cosby asked the court to award him unspecified damages and require public retractions from the women.[^cite_note-Ellis-175]

On February 19, 2016, Cosby's wife [Camille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camille_Cosby) was deposed by plaintiffs after unsuccessfully attempting to stop the deposition. U.S. Magistrate Judge [Mark G. Mastroianni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_G._Mastroianni) ruled that she could [refuse to answer questions about private conversations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spousal_privilege) with her husband.[^cite_note-Camille_ordered_Deposition-176][^cite_note-Camille_ordered_2nDeposition-177][^cite_note-Camille_ordered_2nDeposition_Delay-178] A transcript of the "extremely contentious" deposition was released in May 2016; Camille relied heavily on the marital privilege in refusing to respond to many questions.[^cite_note-CullinsReporter-179] The deposition was interrupted twice as the parties called U.S. District Judge David H. Hennessy to resolve disputes. After the deposition was halted, the court ordered Camille to sit for a second deposition.[^cite_note-CullinsReporter-179]

Lawyers representing the alleged victims planned to depose [Quincy Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy_Jones) who, as a friend and collaborator of Cosby for over fifty years, may have had information vital to the plaintiffs' case.[^cite_note-Quincy_Jones_Deposition-180] Plaintiffs also intend to depose or [subpoena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subpoena) documents from [William Morris Endeavor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Morris_Endeavor), Cosby's former attorneys Singer and Schmitt, and his publicist David Brokaw.[^cite_note-Plaintiff-181]

On March 21, 2016, Judge Anita Brody granted some access to the case file of Constand's lawyer, even though she settled in a confidential agreement with Cosby in 2006. However, Brody did limit the release of the case file to materials pertaining to the seven women plaintiffs and other witnesses.[^cite_note-Constand_settlement_partially_released-182] On September 28, [Joseph Cammarata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Cammarata) filed a motion stating that Cosby violated rules of [civil procedure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_procedure). The motion asked the judge to determine that Cosby was personally responsible for defamatory statements made by his spokespeople and the publication of such materials; and that he be liable for causing the plaintiffs' emotional distress from these comments.[^cite_note-183]

### Janice Dickinson

Dickinson filed a similar defamation lawsuit against Cosby in May 2015 in California. According to Bloom, "Calling Dickinson a liar is a defamatory statement under the law ... and that's the mistake Bill Cosby made."[^cite_note-cnn_dickinson_lawsuit-165] Later court filings include sworn statements from friends and colleagues confirming that Dickinson provided a consistent story for many years about her alleged abuse. [Pablo Fenjves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Fenjves), Dickinson's [ghostwriter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghostwriter), and former [ReganBooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ReganBooks) president [Judith Regan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Regan) asserted that Dickinson came forward with her allegations for her 2002 autobiography, but they were not included in the book because ReganBooks' parent company [HarperCollins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HarperCollins) was afraid of being sued by Cosby.[^cite_note-ladailynews_dickinson-184]

On November 2, 2015, [Los Angeles Superior Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Superior_Court) Judge Debre Katz Weintraub ruled Cosby and Singer must give depositions despite their efforts to have the case thrown out (Singer was not named as a defendant in the case). The ruling stated Dickinson's lawyers could seek answers only about whether the denials were made maliciously, and Cosby and Singer could assert [attorney–client privilege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney%E2%80%93client_privilege) and refuse to answer some questions.[^cite_note-dickinson_deposition-185] Singer was scheduled to be deposed November 19 in Los Angeles and Cosby on November 23 in Boston.[^cite_note-dickinson_deposition_nov23-186] On November 12, a California appeals court temporarily halted the order requiring them to testify. Both parties were required to provide information by the end of the month about why or why not the deposition should proceed. "We are confident that once the Court of Appeals hears full argument on the issues it will allow the deposition of Mr. Cosby and his attorney to go forward," Bloom wrote in an email.[^cite_note-Deposition_Delayed_dickinson-187] Singer announced that he was planning on "pursuing claims against Janice Dickinson and Lisa Bloom after I prevail in this action."[^cite_note-singer_plans_action_dickinson-188]

Dickinson later amended her complaint to name Singer as a co-defendant, alleging that after she made her allegations on CNN, he prepared four [press releases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_release) denying that Cosby drugged and raped her, and calling her allegations "fabricated" and "an outrageous defamatory lie." Singer was later replaced as Cosby's counsel by Christopher Tayback of [Quinn Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinn_Emanuel_Urquhart_&_Sullivan).[^cite_note-dickinson_sues_singer-189] In February 2016, Judge Weintraub granted a motion by the defense to dismiss Singer as a defendant in the lawsuit.[^cite_note-Singer_dismissed_dickinson-190] On March 29, Weintraub denied Cosby's motion to dismiss the case, allowing the case to proceed to a jury trial. Shortly after the ruling, Dickinson said, "I want Bill Cosby in court, I want him to stand under oath."[^cite_note-Cosby_loses_stay_dickinson-191]

On November 21, 2017, in a major blow to both Cosby and Singer, a California appellate court reversed two other courts' decisions to remove Singer as a co-defendant and added him back. In its ruling, the appellate court stated that Singer and Cosby's anti-[SLAPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SLAPP) motion was meritless.[^cite_note-CosbySinger_loses_appeal_dickinson-192] In March 2018, after a last-ditch effort to get the case against Cosby and Singer dismissed, the [California Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Supreme_Court) declined to intervene and hear arguments on the case.[^cite_note-CosbySinger_Supreme_Court_dickinson-193]

In July 2019, Cosby's insurer AIG settled the lawsuit for an undisclosed amount."[^cite_note-Aig_settle_lawsuit_dickinson-194]

### Chloe Goins

On October 6, 2015, Goins filed a lawsuit against Cosby. In her complaint, she alleged that Cosby assaulted her, causing her to suffer "emotional distress," "psychiatric trauma," and impairment of her education. Her lawyer Spencer Kuvin said his client's top priority was that the truth come out and Cosby be criminally prosecuted. Singer stated he would provide documentary evidence to the LAPD that established Cosby was in New York on August 9, 2008, the date of the alleged incident.[^cite_note-cnn.com-146] Although the prosecutor's office had considered filing criminal charges, the charges were dropped because the statute of limitations had expired and a lack of sufficient evidence.[^cite_note-Almasy-101]

In February 2016, Goins dismissed her lawsuit without prejudice.[^cite_note-Goins_Lawsuit2-195] In May 2016, she refiled the suit, adding Playboy founder [Hugh Hefner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugh_Hefner) as a codefendant.[^cite_note-Goins_Lawsuit_Hefner_Cosby-196] The judge denied Cosby's request to dismiss and set the trial for June 2018.[^cite_note-Goins_Lawsuit_Scheduled-197] Cosby's insurance company, American International Group Inc., settled Goins' civil suit against Cosby in April 2019.[^cite_note-198]

### Renita Hill

Hill claims that Cosby gave her a bit part on television, funded her college education, and pledged to assist her career, while sporadically sexually abusing her from 1983 to 1987. In October 2015, she filed suit against Cosby, Singer, and Camille Cosby, alleging defamation, [false light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_light), and [intentional infliction of emotional distress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intentional_infliction_of_emotional_distress).[^cite_note-Renita_Hill_Lawsuit-199] Cosby's attorneys [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Removal_jurisdiction) the case from state to federal court and moved the court to dismiss the suit in late December 2015, arguing that Cosby's denials were opinions protected under the [First Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Amendment).[^cite_note-200] Hill's attorneys responded that Cosby's denials were published facts and hence are defamatory and not covered by First Amendment protections.[^cite_note-201]

The U.S. District Court dismissed the suit "with prejudice" in January 2016, meaning the suit cannot be re-filed. Judge Arthur Schwab decided that statements made by Cosby and his attorneys were opinions protected by the First Amendment.[^cite_note-202] In April 2016, Hill filed an appeal with the [U.S. Court of Appeals for the Third Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Third_Circuit).[^cite_note-203]

### Kristina Ruehli

On November 9, 2015, Ruehli filed a defamation lawsuit against Cosby for denying her claims of rape. Ruehli's allegations against Cosby date back the furthest, with claims that he assaulted her in 1965. She claimed Cosby's vehement denials leveled against the numerous accusers in 2014 constituted defamation. Her complaint states, in part, "It is one thing for an accused sexual assailant to remain silent and allow the legal process, or public opinion, to run its course, but it is quite another for him to unleash his agents to deny that he attacked the plaintiff and other women, to invite others to republish his statements, and to brand them as unreliable liars." In an account from *The New York Times,* Ruehli has dropped her lawsuit without prejudice as of June 24, 2016.[^cite_note-ruehli_suit-204]

### Katherine McKee

On December 22, 2015, McKee, a former girlfriend of late entertainer [Sammy Davis Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sammy_Davis_Jr.), sued Cosby for defamation over claims he and his attorney made about her allegation of being raped in a [Detroit](8687.md) hotel room in the early 1970s. Judge Mastroianni dismissed the lawsuit on February 16, 2017, on the basis of the landmark 1964 [Supreme Court](31737.md) case *[New York Times Co. v. Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times_Co._v._Sullivan),* which requires a [public figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_figure) alleging defamation to show the defendant acted with malice, ruling that McKee was a limited public figure.[^cite_note-McKee_Lawsuit-205] Mckee appealed, but a federal appeals court affirmed Mastroianni's decision to dismiss the lawsuit on December 18, 2017.[^cite_note-McKee_Lawsuit_appeal-206] On October 8, 2017, McKee's appeal was again dismissed; Judge Sandra Lynch cited, "McKee took concerted steps meant to influence the public's perception of whether Cosby was, in fact, a sexual predator," thus rendering herself a public figure. In January 2018, the [U.S. Court of Appeals for the First Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Court_of_Appeals_for_the_First_Circuit) refused to rehear her case in a full court hearing.[^cite_note-207] On petition to the Supreme Court, the Court declined to hear the case in February 2019, re-asserting that the *New York Times Co.* decision was properly applied at all levels.[^cite_note-208]

### Judith Huth

In December 2014, Huth filed a lawsuit alleging sexual assault in 1974 at the Playboy Mansion when she was 15 years old.[^cite_note-Payne-209] It was one of two active lawsuits against Cosby directly alleging sexual assault. Even though the incident occurred more than forty years earlier, California laws allow alleged child sexual abuse victims to bring lawsuits as an adult.[^cite_note-huth_lawsuit_sol-210] Cosby countersued Huth and her attorney Marc Strecker for legal fees.[^cite_note-Cosby_counter_suit-211] Cosby's attorney contended Huth and her attorney engaged in an extortion attempt before filing suit. Singer's claim was made in a notice of [demurrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demurrer). He also sought [sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_sanction) against Huth and Strecker.[^cite_note-212]

On August 4, 2015, a Los Angeles Superior Court judge ordered Cosby to give a sworn deposition in the lawsuit.[^cite_note-Time_underage-213] Another judge refused to dismiss Huth's suit[^cite_note-214] and required him to provide a deposition, which was held on October 9 in [Boston](24437894.md) and lasted 7.5 hours; no further details were made public. The deposition was sealed until at least December 22, 2015.[^cite_note-huth_deposition-215] Allred announced that she would be seeking to depose Cosby again.[^cite_note-Allred_seeks_second_deposition-216]

Huth was scheduled to give her deposition for Cosby's attorneys on January 29, 2016.[^cite_note-Allred_number_accusers_more_coming_forward-166] However, on March 30, Los Angeles Superior Court Judge Craig D. Karlan granted a temporary delay of Cosby's second deposition and any further depositions of Huth. Judge Karlan allowed both sides to continue depositions of other people. Allred said she and partner John West planned to depose other women who claim they were abused by Cosby when underage, and others.[^cite_note-Cosby_2nd_depo_huth_postponed-217] Allred was scheduled to take the deposition of Hill, who claimed Cosby sexually assaulted her when she was 16 years old, on April 8. Allred also announced that she would depose [Hugh Hefner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugh_Hefner) in April.[^cite_note-Hefner_and_Hill_huth_deposed-218]

On April 14, 2016, Cosby's lawyers filed a motion to dismiss Huth's lawsuit claiming that she changed her timeline regarding her "delayed discovery" of psychological injury or illness related to the alleged abuse.[^cite_note-Cosby_tries_to_dismiss_Huth_lawsuit-219] On April 26, Judge Karlan refused to dismiss the majority of Huth's lawsuit; however, he did dismiss a "negligent infliction of emotional distress" claim. "The court is not, at this time, willing to dismiss plaintiff's potentially meritorious claims against defendant based upon mistakes attributable to her former counsel," Karlan wrote. Allred said "We are very happy that the Court agreed and we will continue to vigorously fight for a just result for our client." Cosby spokesperson Monique Pressley did not immediately comment on the decision.[^cite_note-Cosby_tries_to_dismiss_Huth_lawsuit_loses-220]

On September 20, it was revealed that one of the thirteen prosecution witnesses in the criminal trial was alleged Cosby victim Margie Shapiro. Allred filed a motion in the Huth civil case to postpone Shapiro's deposition until after the criminal trial because she believed the defense would try to use the deposition against Shapiro to find discrepancies in the upcoming trial. Cosby attorney Angela Agrusa opposed this motion.[^cite_note-Cosby_Criminal_Trial_Civil_Case_postpone_deposition-221] On June 27, 2017, Judge Karlan set a trial start date for July 30, 2018.[^cite_note-Cosby_Huth_trial_date_set-222]

Two months after Cosby was released from a Pennsylvania prison, the case was revived, and his lawyer said he would continue to plead the 5th. The Los Angeles Superior Court has decided that trial could tentatively occur on April 18, 2022.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224] On March 16, Cosby's lawyers filed court papers alleging that the statute violated the constitution's [ex post facto law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ex_post_facto_law).[^cite_note-225] Two days later, Huth's and Cosby's lawyers had a heated meeting to decide the fate of the lawsuit. Defense lawyer [Jennifer Bonjean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Bonjean) said that they haven't made a decision yet but they were looking for a fair trial somewhere in May.[^cite_note-226] The trial then began setting up the jury on the week of May 23 to 28, and the trial itself is expected to take two weeks to finish. Opening arguments were scheduled for June 1.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228]

On June 7, 2022, Huth testified to a jury in the civil trial, recalling the events that happened in the Playboy Mansion. Notably, photos of a bearded Cosby posing with her date back to 1975, meaning that the incident occurred that year.[^cite_note-229] A day later, Cosby denied these events, saying "I don't know Ms. Huth". He was not expected to attend the trial.[^cite_note-230] On June 21, 2022, the jury ruled in favor of Huth and ordered Cosby pay \$500,000 and no punitive damages.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

### Lili Bernard

On October 14, 2021, Actress Lili Bernard filed a lawsuit under the New Jersey two-year lookback period, which allows sexual assault victimes to sue regardless of when the offense took place. She alleged that Cosby sexually assaulted her on multiple occasions with the most serious allegation in August 1990, when she claims he lured her to the Trump Taj Mahal resort in Atlantic City with a promise to help advance her career but instead drugged and raped her. She alleged that after she woke up the next morning, Cosby threatened her and said that if she contacted the police, he would sue her for defamation and destroy her career. The lawsuit sought \$225 million in damages which includes \$25 million per sexual assault and another \$125 million in punitive damages. Through spokesman Andrew Wyatt, Cosby vehemently denied her accusations and said he would fight the 2-year lookback window as unconstitutional.[^cite_note-233]

### Victoria Valentino

On June 1, 2023, former Playboy model Victoria Valentino, who alleged Cosby drugged and sexually assaulted her at his Los Angeles home in 1969, filed a sex abuse lawsuit in the Los Angeles Superior Court.[^cite_note-californiaextended-234][^cite_note-valentinolawsuit-235] The lawsuit was brought under a new California law which temporarily extended the statute of limitations.[^cite_note-valentinolawsuit-235][^cite_note-californiaextended-234] The lawsuit alleged Cosby gave Valentino and an unnamed friend each a pill while they were having dinner at a steakhouse and that Cosby then drove them to his home, where he then "engaged in forced sexual intercourse" with them.[^cite_note-valentinolawsuit-235][^cite_note-californiaextended-234]

### Morganne Picard

In August 2023, court documents obtained by [People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_\(magazine\)) revealed that singer Marganne Picard filed a lawsuit against Cosby which alleged he drugged and raped her in the late 1980s.[^cite_note-lawsuit1987-1990-236] The lawsuit alleged that Cosby "insisted and encouraged" her to drink beverages that made her feel "extremely intoxicated" on "multiple occasions" between 1987 and 1990.[^cite_note-lawsuit1987-1990-236]

### Joan Tarshis

In November 2023, Joan Tarshis, who was one of Cosby's earliest accusers, filed a lawsuit against Cosby under the New York Survivors Act.[^cite_note-237]

### Nevada lawsuits

On January 30, 2024, Chelan Lasha, who was among the five accusers who testified in Cosby's 2018 criminal trial, filed a civil lawsuit against Cosby in the U.S. District Court in Las Vegas.[^cite_note-2024first-238][^cite_note-lashalawsuit-239] She alleged that he drugged and sexually assault her at a Hilton Hotel in Las Vegas when she was 17.[^cite_note-lashalawsuit-239][^cite_note-2024first-238] She also claimed she first met him when she was 15.[^cite_note-2024first-238] The lawsuit was filed when a change to Nevada law repealed the statute of limitations in civil cases related to sexual assault.[^cite_note-lashalawsuit-239][^cite_note-2024first-238]

Prior to Lasha's lawsuit, nine other women had filed a sexual assault lawsuit against Cosby in Nevada in June 2023.[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-lashalawsuit-239]

## Related litigation

### Suit against Cosby attorney

On November 16, 2015, it was reported that accusers Green and Bowman filed a joint lawsuit against John Schmitt, one of Bill Cosby's lawyers. In November 2014, Schmitt released a statement reading, "Over the last several weeks, decade-old, discredited allegations against Mr. Cosby have resurfaced. The fact they are being repeated does not make them true. Mr. Cosby does not intend to dignify these allegations with any comment." This statement was also posted to the front page of Cosby's official website. Green and Bowman contend that this widely circulated statement amounted to branding them as liars, resulting in emotional distress and other damages.[^cite_note-johnschmitt_lawsuit-241]

### Insurance coverage dispute

AIG Property Casualty Company, Cosby's homeowner's insurer, tentatively agreed to pay Cosby's legal costs for the Green, et al. case in Massachusetts and Dickinson's case in California, over whether these women were defamed when Cosby's representatives denied the occurrence of sexual misconduct. But the insurer has also filed lawsuits related to both cases, seeking [declaratory relief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaratory_relief) that it is not responsible.

Cosby's homeowners insurance does cover him for "personal injury", which is defined in his policy as including "bodily injury"; "shock, emotional distress, mental injury"; "invasion of privacy"; and "defamation, libel, or slander". However, the policy contains an exclusion for "sexual, physical or mental abuse", setting the stage for a potentially novel legal battle over whether a defamation claim about the denial of sexual abuse is covered.[^cite_note-AIG_Lawsuit-11]

In September 2015, Cosby filed a motion to either dismiss AIG's lawsuit or stay it. Cosby's attorneys argued that AIG was acting against Cosby's best interests, and that fighting lawsuits from both his accusers and his insurance company would drain his resources.[^cite_note-reuters_idUSKCN0RF29D20150915-242]

On October 9, 2015, AIG filed a response, calling Cosby's motion "bizarre and possibly unique in the entire history of American jurisprudence", criticizing his lawyers for citing irrelevant case law from other states, and saying that if the court sided with Cosby, it "would amount to a wholesale abandonment of this Court's jurisdiction, all for no apparent reason."[^cite_note-aig_october15-243]

On November 13, 2015, California Federal Judge Beverly O'Connell granted Cosby's motion to dismiss in the Dickinson case and concluded that AIG had a duty to defend. In her opinion, O'Connell looked at the "arising out of" exclusion noted above and declared it ambiguous. "The Court finds that both Plaintiff's broad interpretation and Defendant's narrow interpretation of 'arising out of' are reasonable \[...] The sexual misconduct exclusion could reasonably be read to require that Dickinson's claims merely relate to sexual misconduct, or that Dickinson's claims be proximately caused by the sexual misconduct", she wrote.

Since ambiguous terms are interpreted in favor of coverage, Cosby prevailed. O'Connell gave a second independent reason for finding in Cosby's favor. She concluded the Dickinson complaint contained allegations independent of sexual misconduct. "For example, allegations that Defendant 'intentionally drugged' Dickinson 'even though he knew that she had been in a rehab center for addiction a few months before' could reasonably be interpreted as independent of sexual misconduct, and therefore, within the Policies' coverage ... Similarly, the Dickinson complaint alleges that Defendant's statements contain numerous implications about Dickinson, including the implication that 'Dickinson has copied the claims made publicly by other women against Defendant' and 'the implication that Ms. Dickinson's rape disclosure is a lie and that therefore she is a liar'", she wrote. As courts impose coverage in a "mixed" action, Cosby prevailed here as well but this ruling does not apply to the Massachusetts case, where AIG and Cosby are still fighting to determine who is liable.[^cite_note-Cosby_Dismisses_AIG_Dickinson-244] AIG plans to appeal this ruling.[^cite_note-Cosby_AIG_Appeal_Dickinson-245]

Judge Mark G. Mastroianni denied Cosby's bid to dismiss or pause the AIG suit on December 14, 2015. This judge, also responsible for the Green et al. defamation suit, denied AIG's motion to pause that case.[^cite_note-246]

### Gloria Allred vs. Cobb-Marietta Coliseum, *et al.*

On November 18, 2015, Allred announced she was suing [Cobb County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobb_County,_Georgia), [Georgia](48830.md), Cobb-Marietta Coliseum, and Michael Taormina for violating her First Amendment rights when she was denied entry to Cosby's stand-up comedy performance on May 2, 2015, at the [Cobb Energy Performing Arts Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobb_Energy_Performing_Arts_Center) in [Atlanta](3138.md). Allred participated in a protest outside of the venue, but had also purchased a ticket to the show. Nevertheless, local police officers told her she would be arrested for trespassing if she entered the theater. Allred said Cosby's team coordinated with security and police to deny entry to individuals they had placed on a list of "agitators". Cobb-Marietta Coliseum is the name of the company that organized the show, and Taormina is its managing director. The police officers are employed by Cobb County.

"Performers should not be able to commandeer a police force (as Bill Cosby's representatives appeared to do) in order to exclude individuals from the performance because they have a different viewpoint than the performer has", Allred said. She sought a court order to prohibit what she calls the venue's "censorship policy on admissions".[^cite_note-allred_cobb_co-247]

On September 8, 2016, Allred reached a settlement with the defense. They agreed to pay Allred \$40,000 and agreed not to block any future customers from attending public events held at the Coliseum.[^cite_note-allred_cobb_co_settlement-248]

### Andrea Constand vs. Bruce Castor

On October 26, 2015, Andrea Constand filed a federal lawsuit against former Montgomery County District Attorney Bruce Castor for defamation. Constand is being represented by her original 2006 *Constand vs Cosby* attorney Dolores Troiani.[^cite_note-Contand_sues_Castor-249]

Cosby's lead criminal lawyer in Pennsylvania fought for a subpoena related to his \$2,500 donation to the ex-prosecutor who said he promised Cosby he would never be charged. Lawyer Brian McMonagle co-hosted a political fundraiser for former District Attorney Bruce Castor but said it occurred months before he joined Cosby's defense team. Constand's lawyer instructed McMonagle to detail any ties between his office and Castor, including phone records and documents, and instructed Cosby to do the same.[^cite_note-Contand_sues_Castor_pretrial_hearing-250] At a pretrial hearing on April 15, 2016, a judge ruled that Constand is entitled to any documents shared between Castor and Cosby's attorneys.[^cite_note-251] In January 2019, the two sides reached a confidential settlement.[^cite_note-252]

### Bill Cosby vs. Beverly Johnson

On December 21, 2015, Cosby sued supermodel [Beverly Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Johnson) for defamation,[^cite_note-253] claiming that she told a false story in a *Vanity Fair* article.[^cite_note-254] This was the first time Cosby sued a woman who claimed assault without first being sued himself. The lawsuit accuses Johnson of lying about the incident in which she says Cosby spiked a cup of cappuccino with an unknown drug.[^cite_note-255] Realizing what was happening, Johnson said she screamed and cursed at him several times before Cosby dragged her out and hailed a cab for her. (No sexual contact by Cosby was alleged in Johnson's version of the event.) Allegedly, she also repeated the story in subsequent interviews and her memoirs, released on August 25, 2015.

Cosby's lawsuit seeks unspecified damages and an injunction preventing the model from repeating her claims and requests they be removed from Johnson's memoir.[^cite_note-Cosby_Sues_Beverly_Johnson-256]

On February 19, 2016, Cosby filed a motion to dismiss his lawsuit against Johnson. His attorney Monique Pressley wrote in an email that Cosby decided to focus on his defense in a Pennsylvania criminal case and plans to refile the case before the statute of limitations expires.[^cite_note-Cosby_Drops_lawsuit_Beverly_Johnson-257] No such refiling occurred.

### Bill Cosby vs. Andrea Constand, Constand's mother, Constand's attorneys and *National Enquirer*

On February 1, 2016, Cosby filed a breach of contract lawsuit against Andrea Constand, her mother Gianna Constand, her current lawyer Dolores Troiani, her former lawyer Bebe Kivitz, and the publisher of the *National Enquirer,* a day before the February 2 criminal court hearing that included testimony from Troiani. The lawsuit seeks full repayment plus interest on "the substantial financial benefit". The filing states "Despite being expressly prohibited from disclosing such information to anyone, Andrea Constand volunteered to participate and disclosed such information to the district attorney and others." Troiani has maintained that the settlement agreement barred Constand only from initiating criminal proceedings against Cosby and not from cooperating if authorities approached her first. Prosecutors introduced a copy of the agreement, redacted but for one sentence, at Cosby's pretrial hearing this month. That line read: "Constand agrees that she will not initiate any criminal complaint against Cosby arising from the underlying facts of this case." The lawsuit currently remains under seal and Cosby's lawyers declined to comment on it.[^cite_note-Cosby_sues_for_breach-258]

On July 28, 2016, Cosby filed a motion to dismiss his lawsuit against all defendants.[^cite_note-259]

### Bruce Castor vs. Andrea Constand

On October 20, 2017, Bruce Castor's attorney, James Beasley Jr., announced that he planned to file a lawsuit against Andrea Constand for damages suffered as a result of the election he lost to Kevin Steele, who is now prosecuting Cosby for the same acts Castor would not prosecute him for. He claims Constand conspired with Steele to help him win the election so Cosby could be prosecuted. Constand's attorney, Jeffrey McCarron, responded by saying "If his described basis is the reason for the lawsuit, then we do not expect it will last very long."[^cite_note-Castor_vs_Constand-260]

## Reactions

### Defenses of Cosby

In 2014, [Camille Cosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camille_Cosby), who married Cosby in 1964 when she was 19, released a statement supporting her husband, describing him as a victim of unvetted accusations: "The man I met, and fell in love with, and whom I continue to love, is the man you all knew through his work. He is a kind man ... and a wonderful husband, father and friend."[^cite_note-Karimi-261]

In a January 2015 *Time* magazine article about why black women should stop defending Cosby, actress [Phylicia Rashad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phylicia_Rashad) is quoted defending him: "What you're seeing is the destruction of a legacy. And I think it's orchestrated. I don't know why or who's doing it, but it's the legacy. And it's a legacy that is so important to the culture."[^cite_note-Time_Jones-262]

In a July 2015 *USA Today* article about how the actors of *The Cosby Show* responded to the allegations, [Keshia Knight Pulliam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keshia_Knight_Pulliam) of the cast stated "All I can speak to is the man I know and I love the fact that he has been such an example \[and] you can't take away from the great that he has done, the millions and millions of dollars he has given back to colleges and education, and just what he did with the Cosby Show and how groundbreaking that was. The Cosbys, we were the first family that no matter what race, religion, you saw yourself in." "At the end of the day they are allegations ... I don't have that story to tell."[^cite_note-McDermott-263]

In September 2015, comedian [Damon Wayans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damon_Wayans) attacked the accusers, calling them "un-rape-able", and defended Cosby by stating "It's a money hustle."[^cite_note-Puente_Wayans-264]

In December 2015, actor and comedian [Eddie Griffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Griffin) suggested that Cosby was the victim of a conspiracy to destroy his image and that several other prominent African-American men were victims of similar conspiracies.[^cite_note-265]

*Chicago Tribune* columnist [Clarence Page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Page) writes that the most popular conspiracy theory regarding the allegations involves Cosby being "\[punished] by the powers-that-be for \[his] attempts to buy NBC in the 1990s". Page wrote that the conspiracy does not explain why the conspirators waited so long to make their accusations, with the scandal unfolding many years after Cosby abandoned the purchase. [Dick Gregory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Gregory) was a supporter of this theory.[^cite_note-266]

#### Defenders change their minds

Two notable people who previously defended Cosby and believed in his innocence, changed their minds. American actress and talk show host [Whoopi Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whoopi_Goldberg) and [Joseph C. Phillips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_C._Phillips) (a *Cosby Show* regular for three years) each made public statements on July 15, 2015. Goldberg said "If this is to be tried in the court of public opinion, I got to say all of the information that's out there kind of points to guilt." In an interview, Goldberg had a message for Cosby: "It looks bad, Bill. Either speak up or shut up."[^cite_note-France_Goldberg-267] Goldberg had received threats for staunchly standing by Cosby.[^cite_note-France_Goldberg-267] Goldberg, in the form of a question, referred to Cosby as a "serial rapist" and questioned why he was still on the streets.[^cite_note-Goldberg_serial_rapist-75] Phillips was more direct in a separate comment from Goldberg, saying "Of course Bill Cosby is guilty!"[^cite_note-France_Phillips-268]

### Obama reaction

In July 2015, PAVE: Promoting Awareness and Victim Empowerment, a nonprofit group focusing on sexual assault prevention, launched a [WhiteHouse.gov petition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_the_People_\(petitioning_system\)), calling upon President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) to revoke Cosby's [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom) (which Cosby received from President [George W. Bush](3414021.md) in July 2002).[^cite_note-whitehouse_petition-269] Later the same month, in response to a question at a news conference, President Obama said:

> There's no precedent for revoking a medal. We don't have that mechanism. And, as you know, I tend to make it a policy not to comment on the specifics of cases where there might still be, if not criminal, then civil, issues involved. *\[long pause]* I'll say this, if you give a woman, or a man for that matter, without his or her knowledge, a drug, and then have sex with that person without consent, that's rape. And I think this country, any civilized country, should have no tolerance for rape.[^cite_note-nytob-270]

### Cosby's response

After Tamara Green, a witness in Andrea Constand's case, re-told her story to *Newsweek* in February 2014, Cosby's publicist David Brokaw issued a statement calling it a "10-year discredited accusation that proved to be nothing at the time, and is still nothing."[^cite_note-newsweek_tamara_green-271] When the sexual assault claims against Cosby exploded at the end of 2014, denials by Brokaw and other Cosby representatives became even more vehement, with lawyer Martin Singer calling all the allegations "unsubstantiated, fantastical stories ... \[that] have escalated past the point of absurdity".[^cite_note-people_singer_statement-272]

When Dickinson came forward in November 2014 to accuse Cosby of raping her in 1982, Singer issued a denial on behalf of Cosby, saying, "Janice Dickinson's story accusing Bill Cosby of rape is a lie."[^cite_note-variety_dickinson_lie-273]

Around the time of these interviews, Cosby's lawyers began sending sharply worded letters to publications that wrote about the sexual assault allegations, threatening legal action and using phrases like "proceed at your own peril" if they published certain stories.[^cite_note-lawyer_threats-274] News outlets published the threatening letters from Cosby's attorneys.

In November 2014, one of Cosby's lawyers, John Schmitt, released a statement reading, "Over the last several weeks, decade-old, discredited allegations against Mr. Cosby have resurfaced. The fact they are being repeated does not make them true. Mr. Cosby does not intend to dignify these allegations with any comment." This statement was also posted to the front page of Cosby's official website.[^cite_note-johnschmitt_lawsuit-241]

Subsequent to his arraignment on three felony charges based on the Constand case, Cosby tweeted the following message on December 30, 2015: "Friends and fans, Thank You  \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*]."[^cite_note-275]

#### Interviews

Cosby was asked directly about Buress's comments and the resulting fallout in two November 2014 interviews, which were originally intended to be about a new art exhibit at the Smithsonian featuring his private collection of African American art. In an NPR interview on November 15, reporter [Scott Simon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Simon) said: "This question gives me no pleasure, Mr. Cosby, but there have been serious allegations raised about you in recent days." Cosby became silent, leading to an awkward radio exchange in which Simon verbally described Cosby's actions to listeners: "you're shaking your head no." Simon continued asking Cosby to comment on the allegations before finally wrapping up the interview with no further communication from Cosby.[^cite_note-npr_cosby_interview-276] In a November 6 interview with Associated Press reporter Brett Zongker, Cosby appeared visibly rattled by unexpected Buress questions and told Zongker, "No, no, we don't answer that." In the following recorded minutes, Cosby repeatedly attempted to get Zongker to confirm that the AP would edit out the Buress questions, implying that Zongker's "integrity" and ability to be a "serious reporter" would be compromised if that portion of the interview was not "scuttled". When Zongker did not guarantee this request, Cosby turned to his off-camera publicist David Brokaw and told him to get on the phone with Zongker's editors "immediately".
